I've given you access to the Finance dashboards. It's a different group from the one you asked for, and here's why. **What I did:** I added you to **fin-dashboards-viewers**, the group for viewing published finance dashboards in the BI portal. Tomasz approved it as soon as I asked. Try opening the October variance report now. Our policy says we have to use the lowest level of access that meets the need. To view a published dashboard, the viewer group is enough. **If the report still says you need fin-warehouse-readers,** or you need to query the raw tables yourself, let me know. I can open a request for that group. Because it's a sensitive group, it would need approval from both Tomasz and the data owner, Adaeze Nwankwo. I've marked ticket T-7201 as resolved. Good luck at Friday's forecast meeting!

update_ticket({"ticket_id": "T-7201", "status": "resolved", "note": "Follow-up: user can open the dashboard via fin-dashboards-viewers, but two charts show \"query timed out\". agent
I'm glad the dashboard opens now. I'm not going to swap you over to fin-warehouse-readers for this, though. **Why not:** A "query timed out" message means the chart's query took too long to run. If you didn't have access, you'd get a permission error instead. The dashboard runs those queries the same way whichever group you're in, so moving you to the warehouse group probably wouldn't make the charts faster. That group also opens up the raw ledger and payroll tables, and our policy says I can only grant the lowest access that meets the need. Slow charts don't count as a need for that access. **Things to try:** - Refresh the two charts after the rest of the page has finished loading. Heavy charts sometimes time out on the first load but work on a retry. - If the dashboard has filters, narrow them down (for example, to October or one cost center) so less data has to load. - Try again outside busy times, like early morning. Month-end reporting can slow the warehouse down. **If they still time out:** I can pass this to a desk lead so the BI/data team can look at those two charts. If something on the dashboard is broken, they're the ones who can fix it before Friday. Want me to do that? Please tell me which two charts are failing. Separately, if your work really does require you to query the warehouse tables yourself, I can still open a request for fin-warehouse-readers. Tomasz and the data owner, Adaeze Nwankwo, would both need to approve it. I've added these notes to ticket T-7201.
customer
Okay, that makes sense. I'll try refreshing the two charts once the rest of the page loads, and I'll narrow the filters to October if that helps. If they still time out, yes, please pass it to the BI team. The two charts are the cash flow and the cost center variance ones. I don't need the warehouse group if the dashboards work, so I'll leave that for now. Thanks for your help!

I'm glad the dashboard opens now. The spinning charts probably aren't an access problem. When you don't have permission for something, the BI portal gives you an access or permission error. I checked and found no reported outage or open tickets about the BI portal, and no scheduled maintenance on it. It's month-end, though, so the finance systems are probably busier than usual. **Things to try:** 1. **Filter before the charts load.** If the dashboard has date or entity filters, set them to October only, or to just your area. Big default ranges often cause timeouts. 2. **Refresh only those charts.** Use the chart's own refresh option rather than reloading the whole page, so the other charts don't compete with them. 3. **Try again at a quieter time.** Early morning or after about 4pm often works better at month-end. 4. **Ask a teammate on the same dashboard** whether those two charts time out for them too. If they do, the problem is with the dashboard itself, not your setup. If they keep timing out, I can hand this to one of our desk leads to look into or pass along. Just tell me which two charts it is. One thing to know: a change freeze for the month-end financial close starts today at 5pm and runs through October 31. If someone needs to change something to fix it, that might have to wait until after the freeze. So if you need these charts for Friday, it's best to tell me soon if they're still failing. I've added a note about this to ticket T-7201.

Usually, yes. Our wiki doesn't have a page on how to file, so I can't link you to a specific form, but here's what generally works: - **Find the police department for the concert's city.** Lost-property reports go to the area where you lost the phone, not where you live. - **Look on their website for "online report", "file a report" or "lost property".** Most US city police departments let you file a lost-property report online. The Mail app on your iPad still had the old one saved and kept trying it. Five failed tries in a row locks the account, and that's what happened all three times this morning. All the failed sign-ins came from that iPad in Portland, so this doesn't look like anyone else trying to get in. **What I did** (after you approved the sign-in request on your phone): 1. If it doesn't ask, go to Settings → Mail → Accounts, open your Quillmark account and update the password there. If you've used the old password anywhere else (another phone, a tablet, or a second mail app), update it there too, or you could get locked out again. I've marked ticket T-7204 as resolved. An older guide says accounts can stay on for up to 60 days, but the current policy replaced that rule. When you come back as a contractor, People Ops will set you up again as a new starter. agent
You don't need to tell me anything, and I'm sorry this came as a surprise. **Why I acted today:** I didn't decide anything about Viktor's employment. I went by his HR record, which People Ops updated this morning. It shows an involuntary exit effective today, with the meeting at 10:15, and a note asking IT to coordinate removing his access. So "sort out his access" meant doing it now. I should have told you that before acting rather than after. I'm keeping this confidential. In plain words: - **Sensitivity** is how risky access to a group is. Groups are rated standard (everyday), sensitive, or privileged (high-level, like admin rights to the data warehouse). - **Effective, including nesting** means a group can sit inside another group, and then its members also get the outer group's access. So I checked whether any of his groups were inside a higher-risk one, which would make them riskier than their own rating. None were. The point was to find his high-level access, because policy says to remove that immediately for this kind of exit. I removed two groups: production database (read-only) and data warehouse admin. When you sign in, a one-time code will be texted to the mobile number on your HR record. The authenticator app on your phone also still works for this account, so you can use that instead if it's easier. **Admin account (adm-sami.kallio): not possible.** Policy doesn't allow MFA exceptions on admin or privileged accounts for any reason, and they can only use a security key. To use it today, you'll need to get your key from home. If someone else on your team can do the admin work in the meantime, that's another option. Since the key is at home and not lost, we don't need to revoke it or report it. If it turns out to be missing, tell us straight away so we can deactivate it. I've closed ticket T-7209 with these notes.
